<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that, for all integers <i>n</i> ≥ 3 and <i>k</i> ≥ 1 and all positive real capacities $`R_1,\ldots,R_k`$, the closed standard symplectic $`2n`$-balls of these capacities embed disjointly into the interior of a ball of capacity <i>R</i> &gt; 0 if and only if
> 
> $`\displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^k R_i^n\lt R^n, \qquad R_i+R_j\lt R\quad(i\ne j).`$
> 
> Capacity is <i>π</i> times the squared Euclidean radius, and each embedding is defined on a neighborhood of its closed source ball. This proves Siegel and Yao's Conjecture A.

- **claim-main-packing-criterion** (classification, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): Let $n\ge3$ and $k\ge1$ be integers. For positive real numbers $R,R_1,\ldots,R_k$, there exists a symplectic embedding $$\bigsqcup_{i=1}^k B^{2n}(R_i) \hookrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{int}}B^{2n}(R)$$ if and only if $$\label{intro:inequalities} \sum_{i=1}^k R_i^n<R^n, \qquad R_i+R_j<R\quad (1\le i<j\le k).$$
  - Explanation: For complex dimension at least three, a finite family of closed balls embeds disjointly into an open ball exactly when the volume inequality and the pairwise two-ball inequality both hold.
- **claim-hamiltonian-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-comparison)): Let $P:\Delta\to\mathbb R$ be continuous and concave. Suppose that its Lebesgue mean $\overline P$ is positive and, for some $0<\tau<1$, $$\label{cmp:outer-positive} \inf_{\Delta\setminus\tau\Delta}P>0.$$ Then there are a smooth toric function $K$ and a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism $q:V\to V$ such that $$\label{cmp:conclusion} (K-P)(qu)<K(u)\qquad(u\in V).$$ The map $q$ admits a Hamiltonian isotopy from the identity preserving $V$, whose Hamiltonians Poisson commute with $b_\nu\cdot p$ near each corresponding outer face, uniformly in time.
  - Explanation: A concave profile with positive mean that is positive outside a radial core can be turned into a strict pointwise inequality by a Hamiltonian change of fiber coordinates, with control at the outer faces.
- **claim-rearrangement-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-rearrangement-properties)): Each $R_j$ maps continuous convex functions on $\Delta$ to continuous convex functions. It preserves Lebesgue integrals, order, and addition of constants, and is nonexpansive in uniform norm. Its output is nondecreasing in $p_j$; moreover it preserves being nondecreasing in any other coordinate. Consequently $R=R_m\cdots R_1$ has coordinatewise nondecreasing output.
  - Explanation: The coordinatewise increasing rearrangements keep convexity, preserve integrals and order, are nonexpansive in the uniform norm, and produce monotone output.
- **claim-lifted-rearrangement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-lifted)): For every continuous convex $F:\Delta\to\mathbb R$, every coordinate $j$, and every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism $q_j:V\to V$ such that $$F\circ q_j\le R_jF+\varepsilon.$$ Its isotopy preserves $V$, and its Hamiltonians Poisson commute with every outer-face moment near the corresponding face.
  - Explanation: One coordinate rearrangement is realized up to any prescribed error by a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism of the toric domain that respects the outer faces.
- **claim-nested-disks** (construction, [source](#stmt-cmp-planar-disks)): Let $Y$ be a compact convex subset of a Euclidean space, let $0<a_1<\cdots<a_l<A$, and let $$E_1(y)\Subset\mathop{\mathrm{int}}E_2(y)\Subset\cdots \Subset\mathop{\mathrm{int}}E_l(y)\Subset D(A) \qquad(y\in Y)$$ be smooth families of embedded closed disks of areas $a_1,\ldots,a_l$. There is a smooth family of Hamiltonian isotopies of $D(A)$, supported in a common compact subset, whose endpoint maps carry each centered disk of area $a_j$ onto $E_j(y)$.
  - Explanation: Smooth families of nested embedded planar disks of the right areas are reached from centered disks by a smooth family of compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopies.
- **claim-planar-rearrangement** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-disk-realization)): Let $f_y:[0,1]\to\mathbb R$ be a jointly continuous family of convex functions, with $y$ in a compact convex parameter set. Let $g_y$ be their increasing rearrangements with respect to Lebesgue measure. For every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth family of compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopies of $D(1)$, with endpoints $\psi_y$, such that $$f_y\bigl(\pi\|\psi_y(z)\|^2\bigr) \le g_y(\pi\|z\|^2)+\varepsilon \qquad(z\in\overline{D(1)}).$$ Their supports lie in a common compact subset of $D(1)$.
  - Explanation: The increasing rearrangement of a continuous family of convex functions on an interval is approximated within any error by Hamiltonian disk maps depending smoothly on parameters.
- **claim-relative-area-primitives** (construction, [source](#stmt-surf-primitive)): Let $\Sigma$ be a compact connected oriented surface with nonempty boundary. A smooth family $\eta_p$ of two-forms, supported in a fixed compact subset of $\mathop{\mathrm{int}}(\Sigma)$ and satisfying $\int_\Sigma\eta_p=0$, admits a smooth family of one-forms $\alpha_p$, supported in a fixed compact subset of $\mathop{\mathrm{int}}(\Sigma)$, with $\mathrm d_C\alpha_p=\eta_p$. The choice can be made by a fixed linear operator on the two-forms.
  - Explanation: Families of zero-integral compactly supported two-forms on a bordered surface have compactly supported primitives chosen by a fixed linear operator.
- **claim-moser-outer-faces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-surf-moser)): Let $\Sigma$ be a compact connected oriented surface with nonempty boundary. Let $\Omega_\lambda$, $0\leq\lambda\leq1$, be a smooth family of symplectic forms on neighborhoods of $\Sigma\times V$, and suppose $\partial_\lambda\Omega_\lambda=\mathrm d\alpha_\lambda$, where $\alpha_\lambda$ is horizontal and vanishes in a fixed collar of $\partial\Sigma\times V$. Suppose, near each outer face and uniformly in $\lambda$, that its standard Hamiltonian vector field satisfies $$\iota_{X_{\mu_\nu}}\Omega_\lambda=-\mathrm d\mu_\nu.$$ Then the Moser isotopy preserves $\Sigma\times V$ and its interior, is the identity near the base boundary, and is defined on a neighborhood of the compact region.
  - Explanation: A Moser isotopy driven by a horizontal primitive preserves the product domain, its interior and the base boundary collar when the outer-face Hamiltonian condition holds.
- **claim-surface-packing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-surf-packing)): Let $C^o$ be an oriented surface obtained from a closed connected surface of genus at least one by removing finitely many, but at least one, points. Let $\Sigma_\ell\subset\mathop{\mathrm{int}}(\Sigma_{\ell+1})$ be an exhaustion by compact connected surfaces with smooth nonempty boundary. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large $\ell$, a neighborhood of $\Sigma_\ell\times V$ carries a form $$\Omega_\ell=\omega_V+\mathrm d\Gamma_\ell,\qquad \mathrm d_C\Gamma_\ell=\kappa_\ell(p)>0,$$ where $\Gamma_\ell$ is toric horizontal. Assume that $$A_\ell(p):=\int_{\Sigma_\ell}\kappa_\ell(p) \longrightarrow H(p) \quad\hbox{uniformly on }\Delta,$$ where $H$ is continuous and positive. The models for different $\ell$ need not be compatible.* *Let $r_1,\ldots,r_k>0$ satisfy $$\{p\geq0:\textstyle\sum_jp_j\leq r_i\}\subset\Delta, \qquad \{p\geq0:\textstyle\sum_jp_j\leq r_i\} \cap\{\mu_\nu=c_\nu\}=\varnothing \quad\hbox{for every }i,\nu.$$ Suppose the function $$P(p)=H(p)-C_r(p),\qquad C_r(p)=\sum_{i=1}^k(r_i-\textstyle\sum_jp_j)_+,$$ is concave, has positive Lebesgue mean on $\Delta$, and is bounded below by a positive constant on $\Delta\setminus\tau\Delta$ for some $0<\tau<1$. Then, for every choice $0<r_i'<r_i$, some model contains a disjoint symplectic packing of the closed balls $B^{2m+2}(r_i')$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{int}}(\Sigma_\ell\times V)$. Each embedding is defined on a neighborhood of its closed ball.
  - Explanation: Over a positive-genus punctured surface with suitable limiting area profile, balls of capacities slightly below the auxiliary ones pack disjointly into a finite model.
- **claim-relative-kahler-moser** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-relative-moser)): Let $X$ be a compact complex manifold and let $\omega_0,\omega_1$ be cohomologous Kähler forms. Let $S_1,\ldots,S_e$ be pairwise disjoint closed smooth complex submanifolds. There is an isotopy $\psi_t$ of $X$, starting at the identity, such that $$\psi_1^*\omega_1=\omega_0,\qquad \psi_t(S_j)=S_j.$$ If a compact Lie group $G$ acts holomorphically on $X$, the forms are $G$-invariant, and every $S_j$ is $G$-invariant, the isotopy can be chosen $G$-equivariant. It then preserves every connected component of the fixed locus $X^G$, whether or not that component meets any $S_j$.
  - Explanation: Cohomologous Kähler forms are related by an isotopy preserving given disjoint complex submanifolds, equivariantly under a compact group action.
- **claim-projective-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-projective-transfer)): Let $f:\mathcal X\to B$ be a flat projective family over a smooth algebraic curve over $\mathbb C$, with a marked point $0$, and let $\mathcal L$ be a relatively ample rational polarization. Let $Y$ be a smooth projective irreducible component of $\mathcal X_0$, or a union of pairwise disjoint smooth projective irreducible components. The following statements hold after replacing $B$ by a Zariski-open neighborhood of $0$; for symplectic transport we subsequently work in a small complex analytic neighborhood.* 1. *For a sufficiently divisible and sufficiently large $N$, a full basis of $H^0(Y,\mathcal L^N\|_Y)$ can be extended to sections defining a relative projective embedding, with additional sections whose restrictions to $Y$ vanish. Consequently one can choose a relative Kähler form whose restriction to $Y$ is the projective form from that full basis, divided by $N$. If a complex torus $(\mathbb C^*)^r$ acts holomorphically on $Y$ and its action is linearized on $\mathcal L^N\|_Y$, the basis may be chosen by weights, making the restricted form invariant under the compact torus $(S^1)^r$.* 2. *Let $K\subset Y$ be compact and contained in the smooth submersion locus of $f$. For the chosen form there is, for all sufficiently small nonzero $t$, a symplectic embedding of a neighborhood of $K$ into the fiber $\mathcal X_t$. If $K$ avoids a specified closed subset of $\mathcal X$, the embedding can be chosen with image avoiding that subset.* 3. *If a compact group acts on the family over $B$ and the total form is invariant, this transport is equivariant. In particular, for a Hamiltonian circle action, it preserves moment functions up to an additive constant on each connected transport domain. If one transported point has the same prescribed moment value in both fibers, this constant is zero.
  - Explanation: Compact parts of a smooth central component of a projective degeneration transport symplectically to nearby fibers, avoiding closed sets and respecting torus symmetry and moments.
- **claim-normal-cone-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-transfer)): Let $T$ be a smooth projective manifold, $Z\subset T$ a smooth closed submanifold of positive codimension, possibly disconnected, and $L$ an ample rational polarization. Let $$T'=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_ZT,\qquad Y=\mathbb P_Z(\mathbf1\oplus N_{Z/T}),\qquad D_\infty=\mathbb P_Z(N_{Z/T})\subset Y.$$ Write $E_Z$ for the exceptional divisor on $T'$; when $Z$ is a divisor, $T'\simeq T$ and $E_Z$ means $Z$. Suppose $d>0$ is rational and $$\pi^*L-dE_Z\quad\hbox{on }T', \qquad L\|_Z+dU\quad\hbox{on }Y$$ are ample.* *Let $F\subset T$ be a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth closed complex submanifolds. Assume the inverse image of $F\cap Z$ under $Y\to Z$ is also a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth complex submanifolds; the empty union is allowed. Let $\omega_T$ be any Kähler form in class $L$, and let $\omega_Y$ be a Kähler form in class $L\|_Z+dU$, invariant under scalar rotation of the normal summand. Then every compact subset of $$Y\setminus\bigl(D_\infty\cup\pi_Y^{-1}(F\cap Z)\bigr)$$ has a neighborhood admitting a symplectic embedding into $(T\setminus F,\omega_T)$, with its given form $\omega_Y$. In particular any compact packing there transfers with neighborhood embeddings.
  - Explanation: Compact subsets of the normal-bundle component of the normal cone embed symplectically into the original manifold away from the chosen divisors.
- **claim-equal-normal-summands** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-bundle)): Let $A$ be an ample line bundle on a smooth projective manifold $Z$, equipped with a Hermitian metric of Chern form $\sigma>0$. Suppose $$N=A^b\oplus\cdots\oplus A^b$$ has $\ell$ summands, where $b$ is a positive integer. If $d>0$ and $bd<1$, the class $\sigma+dU$ on $Y=\mathbb P_Z(\mathbf1\oplus N)$ has an invariant Kähler representative with the following affine description: $$\label{geo:bundle-form} \omega_Y=\sigma+\mathrm d\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}p_j\alpha_j\right), \qquad p_j\ge0,\quad \sum_jp_j<d.$$ Here $\alpha_j$ is the angular connection of period one on the $j$-th normal line, with curvature $-b\sigma$, and the fiber coordinates have standard moments $p_j$. The expression $p_j\alpha_j$ is smooth across its zero axis. For rational $d$, this also proves ampleness of the indicated rational polarization.* *If $Z=C$ is a curve, then over a compact bordered subsurface $\Sigma$ the model, truncated on any compact moment polytope strictly inside $\sum p_j<d$, admits a toric horizontal primitive as in Lemma <a href="#surf:packing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="surf:packing">9</a>, with $$\kappa(p)=\left(1-b\sum_jp_j\right)\sigma.$$ For bordered exhaustions of $C$ minus finitely many points, the integrated areas converge uniformly on such a polytope to $$H(p)=(\deg A)\left(1-b\sum_jp_j\right).$$
  - Explanation: The projective completion of a sum of equal positive normal line bundles carries an explicit invariant Kähler form whose horizontal curvature gives a computable area profile.
- **claim-blowup-shell** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-shell)): Let $n\ge2$, let $o\in\mathbb P^n\setminus H_\infty$, and write $$T=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_o\mathbb P^n,\qquad H=\pi^*c_1(\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^n}(1)).$$ Let $E$ be the exceptional divisor and use $H_\infty$ also for its proper transform. For $0<a<1$, there is a Kähler form $\omega_a$ in class $H-aE$ for which $$(T\setminus(E\cup H_\infty),\omega_a) \simeq \left(\left\{z\in\mathbb C^n: a<\pi\sum_j\|z_j\|^2<1\right\},\omega_0\right).$$ The same symplectomorphism type holds for every Kähler form in this class. In addition, $AH-BE$ is ample as a rational polarization whenever $A>B>0$ are rational.
  - Explanation: The complement of the exceptional divisor and the hyperplane at infinity in the blowup of projective space at a point is symplectomorphic to an open spherical shell.
- **claim-quadric-curves** (construction, [source](#stmt-app-quadrics)): Let $N\ge3$, and let $F\subset\mathbb P^N$ be a hyperplane. There is a smooth connected curve $C$, the complete intersection of $N-1$ quadrics, transverse to $F$. If $A=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^N}(1)\|_C$, then $$\label{app:quadric-data} \deg A=2^{N-1},\qquad N_{C/\mathbb P^N}=(A^{\otimes2})^{\oplus(N-1)},\qquad g(C)=1+(N-3)2^{N-2}.$$ Writing $H$ for the hyperplane pullback and $E_C$ for the exceptional divisor of $\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_C\mathbb P^N$, the rational class $H-dE_C$ is ample whenever $0<d<1/2$. The class $$A+dU\quad\hbox{on}\quad \mathbb P_C\bigl(\mathbf1\oplus (A^{\otimes2})^{\oplus(N-1)}\bigr)$$ is ample for the same range of rational $d$.
  - Explanation: A smooth connected complete intersection of quadrics transverse to a hyperplane exists, with computed degree, normal bundle and genus, and ample classes in a range of parameters.
- **claim-small-capacities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-app-small)): Let $n\ge3$, and let $\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_k$ be positive real numbers with $$\rho_i<\tfrac12\quad(1\le i\le k),\qquad \sum_{i=1}^k\rho_i^n<1.$$ Then the closed balls of capacities $\rho_i$ admit a disjoint symplectic packing into $\mathop{\mathrm{int}}B^{2n}(1)$, with embeddings defined on neighborhoods of the closed source balls.
  - Explanation: When all capacities are below one half and the volume inequality holds, the balls pack into the unit ball.
- **claim-large-ball-high-dimension** (theorem, [source](#stmt-app-large)): Let $n\ge4$, let $A\ge1/2$, and let $\rho_1,\ldots,\rho_k>0$ satisfy $$A^n+\sum_i\rho_i^n<1,\qquad A+\rho_i<1\quad(1\le i\le k).$$ Then the closed ball of capacity $A$ and the closed balls of capacities $\rho_i$ admit a disjoint symplectic packing into $\mathop{\mathrm{int}}B^{2n}(1)$, with embeddings on neighborhoods of all source balls.
  - Explanation: For complex dimension at least four, one ball of capacity at least one half together with smaller balls packs under the volume and pairwise inequalities.
- **claim-six-quadric-blowup** (classification, [source](#stmt-six-quadric)): There is an identification $D=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_{q_1,q_2}\mathbb P^2$, for two distinct points $q_1,q_2$. Write $M$ for the pullback of the plane hyperplane class and $e_i$ for the exceptional divisor class over $q_i$. Under this identification, $$\label{six:quadric-classes} H\|_D=2M-e_1-e_2,\qquad E\|_D=M-e_1-e_2.$$ Consequently $$\label{six:normal-class} N_{D/T}=\mathcal O_D(3M-e_1-e_2),\qquad L\|_D=3bM-s(e_1+e_2).$$ For every rational $0<c<s$, deformation to the normal cone of $D$ has ample component classes $$(1-2c)H-(a-c)E \quad\hbox{and}\quad 3bM-s(e_1+e_2)+cU$$ on its strict component and on $$\label{six:Y} Y=\mathbb P_D\bigl(\mathbf1\oplus\mathcal O_D(3M-e_1-e_2)\bigr),$$ respectively. The intersections of $D$ with $E$ and $H_\infty$ are disjoint smooth curves; their images in $\mathbb P^2$ are a line $\ell$ and a smooth conic $Q$, both through $q_1,q_2$.
  - Explanation: The strict transform of the chosen quadric is a plane blown up at two points, with explicit restricted divisor classes, normal bundle and ample component classes.
- **claim-six-cut** (theorem, [source](#stmt-six-cut)): Suppose $s<d_1<b$ and $\widehat V$ has an invariant Kähler form in the class $$\label{six:Vhat-class} A=3bM+d_1U_1-s(\widehat E_1+\widehat E_2).$$ Normalize its first moment $p_1$ to be zero on the strict bottom section $D=\mathop{\mathrm{Bl}}_{q_1,q_2}\mathbb P^2$. For $0<c<s$, the cut retaining $p_1\le c$ is the complex projective bundle $Y$ of <a href="#six:Y" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="six:Y">[six:Y]</a>, with an invariant Kähler form in class $$3bM-s(e_1+e_2)+cU.$$ Its open retained region is symplectically identified with $\{p_1<c\}\subset\widehat V$, and this identification preserves the projection to $D$ on the attracting basin of the bottom section. In particular it preserves avoidance of inverse images of $\ell\cup Q$ in the plane.
  - Explanation: A lower circle cut of the auxiliary threefold realizes the projective bundle over the quadric blowup with the required Kähler class, preserving projection to the base.
- **claim-six-cubic-ampleness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-six-cubic-ampleness)): Both classes in <a href="#six:component-classes" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="six:component-classes">[six:component-classes]</a> are ample. Consequently $\mathcal A$ is relatively ample near zero.
  - Explanation: Both component classes of the cubic degeneration are ample, so the relative class is relatively ample near zero.
- **claim-six-weights** (theorem, [source](#stmt-six-weights)): For the above full projective system, the weight $(k_1,k_2)$ runs over $$0\le k_1\le Nd_1,\qquad 0\le k_2\le Nd_2-k_1.$$ Its coefficient space on $C$ is $$\label{six:weight-space} H^0\!\left(C,\, \mathcal O_C((3bN-3k_1-3k_2)M) \bigl(-m_kq_1-m_kq_2\bigr)\right), \qquad m_k=(sN-k_1-k_2)_+.$$ For sufficiently large divisible $N$, these systems after removing the indicated zeros are all basepoint-free.
  - Explanation: The weight ranges and coefficient spaces of the full projective system are described, and after removing forced zeros the systems are basepoint-free for large divisible scaling.
- **claim-six-toric-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-six-toric-model)): Let $\Delta$ be a compact downward polytope strictly below the two upper boundaries of $\mathcal Q$, and let $$C^\circ=C\setminus\{q_1,q_2\}.$$ Over every compact bordered surface $\Sigma\subset C^\circ$, the corresponding affine moment region has smooth product disk coordinates, including their lower axes, in which its form is $$\label{six:surface-form} \Omega=\omega_V+\mathrm d\Gamma,\qquad \mathrm d_C\Gamma=\kappa(p)>0.$$ Here $\Gamma$ is horizontal and toric, with smooth extensions to neighborhoods of $\Sigma$ and $\Delta$. For an exhaustion of $C^\circ$ by such surfaces, the integrated areas converge uniformly on $\Delta$ to $$\label{six:area} H_0(p)=9(b-p_1-p_2)-2(s-p_1-p_2)_+.$$ The same limit and uniform convergence hold after deleting any additional finite set of base points.
  - Explanation: Over bordered pieces of the punctured cubic the model has product disk coordinates with positive base curvature, and the integrated areas converge uniformly to the explicit concave profile.
- **claim-six-moment-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-six-moment-transfer)): Let $\Sigma$ be a connected compact bordered surface in the unmarked base, and let $K$ be the entire compact moment region over $\Sigma$ corresponding to a full-dimensional downward polytope $\Delta$ as in Lemma <a href="#six:toric-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="six:toric-model">22</a>, strictly below the two upper faces of $\mathcal Q$. For sufficiently small nonzero parameter, symplectic parallel transport from $W$ to $\widehat V$ is defined on a neighborhood of $K$ and preserves $p_1$ exactly, when the moments on both components are zero on their first bottom sections. If $K$ avoids the inverse image of a closed subset of the plane under the family map, its image has the same avoidance for sufficiently small parameter.
  - Explanation: Parallel transport from the cubic component to a nearby fiber preserves the first moment exactly and preserves avoidance of closed subsets of the plane.
- **claim-six-large** (theorem, [source](#stmt-six-large)): Let $R_0,R_1,\ldots,R_k>0$, with $R_0\ge1/2$, satisfy $$\sum_{i=0}^k R_i^3<1,\qquad R_i+R_j<1\quad(i\ne j).$$ Then the disjoint union of their closed six-dimensional balls embeds symplectically into the open ball of capacity one, with each embedding defined on a neighborhood of its closed source.
  - Explanation: In complex dimension three, with one capacity at least one half, the volume and pairwise inequalities suffice for a disjoint packing into the unit ball.

Let $g_y$ be their increasing rearrangements with respect to Lebesgue measure. For every $\\varepsilon>0$, there is a smooth family of compactly supported Hamiltonian isotopies of $D(1)$, with endpoints $\\psi_y$, such that $$f_y\\bigl(\\pi|\\psi_y(z)|^2\\bigr)\n \\le g_y(\\pi|z|^2)+\\varepsilon\n \\qquad(z\\in\\overline{D(1)}).$$ Their supports lie in a common compact subset of $D(1)$.",
```json
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-surf-primitive",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Sigma$ be a compact connected oriented surface with nonempty boundary.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-surf-moser",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Sigma$ be a compact connected oriented surface with nonempty boundary. Let $\\Omega_\\lambda$, $0\\leq\\lambda\\leq1$, be a smooth family of symplectic forms on neighborhoods of $\\Sigma\\times V$, and suppose $\\partial_\\lambda\\Omega_\\lambda=\\mathrm d\\alpha_\\lambda$, where $\\alpha_\\lambda$ is horizontal and vanishes in a fixed collar of $\\partial\\Sigma\\times V$. Suppose, near each outer face and uniformly in $\\lambda$, that its standard Hamiltonian vector field satisfies $$\\iota_{X_{\\mu_\\nu}}\\Omega_\\lambda=-\\mathrm d\\mu_\\nu.$$ Then the Moser isotopy preserves $\\Sigma\\times V$ and its interior, is the identity near the base boundary, and is defined on a neighborhood of the compact region.",
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-surf-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C^o$ be an oriented surface obtained from a closed connected surface of genus at least one by removing finitely many, but at least one, points. Let $\\Sigma_\\ell\\subset\\mathop{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Sigma_{\\ell+1})$ be an exhaustion by compact connected surfaces with smooth nonempty boundary. Suppose that, for all sufficiently large $\\ell$, a neighborhood of $\\Sigma_\\ell\\times V$ carries a form $$\\Omega_\\ell=\\omega_V+\\mathrm d\\Gamma_\\ell,\\qquad\n \\mathrm d_C\\Gamma_\\ell=\\kappa_\\ell(p)>0,$$ where $\\Gamma_\\ell$ is toric horizontal. Assume that $$A_\\ell(p):=\\int_{\\Sigma_\\ell}\\kappa_\\ell(p)\n       \\longrightarrow H(p)\n       \\quad\\hbox{uniformly on }\\Delta,$$ where $H$ is continuous and positive. The models for different $\\ell$ need not be compatible.*\n\n*Let $r_1,\\ldots,r_k>0$ satisfy $$\\{p\\geq0:\\textstyle\\sum_jp_j\\leq r_i\\}\\subset\\Delta,\n \\qquad\n \\{p\\geq0:\\textstyle\\sum_jp_j\\leq r_i\\}\n       \\cap\\{\\mu_\\nu=c_\\nu\\}=\\varnothing\n       \\quad\\hbox{for every }i,\\nu.$$ Suppose the function $$P(p)=H(p)-C_r(p),\\qquad\n C_r(p)=\\sum_{i=1}^k(r_i-\\textstyle\\sum_jp_j)_+,$$ is concave, has positive Lebesgue mean on $\\Delta$, and is bounded below by a positive constant on $\\Delta\\setminus\\tau\\Delta$ for some $0<\\tau<1$. Then, for every choice $0<r_i'<r_i$, some model contains a disjoint symplectic packing of the closed balls $B^{2m+2}(r_i')$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{int}}(\\Sigma_\\ell\\times V)$. Each embedding is defined on a neighborhood of its closed ball.",
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-relative-moser",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a compact complex manifold and let $\\omega_0,\\omega_1$ be cohomologous Kähler forms. Let $S_1,\\ldots,S_e$ be pairwise disjoint closed smooth complex submanifolds. There is an isotopy $\\psi_t$ of $X$, starting at the identity, such that $$\\psi_1^*\\omega_1=\\omega_0,\\qquad \\psi_t(S_j)=S_j.$$ If a compact Lie group $G$ acts holomorphically on $X$, the forms are $G$-invariant, and every $S_j$ is $G$-invariant, the isotopy can be chosen $G$-equivariant. It then preserves every connected component of the fixed locus $X^G$, whether or not that component meets any $S_j$.",
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-relative-moser",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a compact complex manifold and let $\\omega_0,\\omega_1$ be cohomologous Kähler forms. It then preserves every connected component of the fixed locus $X^G$, whether or not that component meets any $S_j$.",
          "source_id": "PM-343.1"

It then preserves every connected component of the fixed locus $X^G$, whether or not that component meets any $S_j$.",
      "explanation": "Cohomologous Kähler forms are related by an isotopy preserving given disjoint complex submanifolds, equivariantly under a compact group action.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-projective-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\mathcal X\\to B$ be a flat projective family over a smooth algebraic curve over $\\mathbb C$, with a marked point $0$, and let $\\mathcal L$ be a relatively ample rational polarization. Let $Y$ be a smooth projective irreducible component of $\\mathcal X_0$, or a union of pairwise disjoint smooth projective irreducible components. The following statements hold after replacing $B$ by a Zariski-open neighborhood of $0$; for symplectic transport we subsequently work in a small complex analytic neighborhood.*\n\n1.  *For a sufficiently divisible and sufficiently large $N$, a full basis of $H^0(Y,\\mathcal L^N|_Y)$ can be extended to sections defining a relative projective embedding, with additional sections whose restrictions to $Y$ vanish. Consequently one can choose a relative Kähler form whose restriction to $Y$ is the projective form from that full basis, divided by $N$. If a complex torus $(\\mathbb C^*)^r$ acts holomorphically on $Y$ and its action is linearized on $\\mathcal L^N|_Y$, the basis may be chosen by weights, making the restricted form invariant under the compact torus $(S^1)^r$.*\n\n2.  *Let $K\\subset Y$ be compact and contained in the smooth submersion locus of $f$. For the chosen form there is, for all sufficiently small nonzero $t$, a symplectic embedding of a neighborhood of $K$ into the fiber $\\mathcal X_t$. If $K$ avoids a specified closed subset of $\\mathcal X$, the embedding can be chosen with image avoiding that subset.*\n\n3.  *If a compact group acts on the family over $B$ and the total form is invariant, this transport is equivariant. In particular, for a Hamiltonian circle action, it preserves moment functions up to an additive constant on each connected transport domain. If one transported point has the same prescribed moment value in both fibers, this constant is zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-projective-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\mathcal X\\to B$ be a flat projective family over a smooth algebraic curve over $\\mathbb C$, with a marked point $0$, and let $\\mathcal L$ be a relatively ample rational polarization. Let $Y$ be a smooth projective irreducible component of $\\mathcal X_0$, or a union of pairwise disjoint smooth projective irreducible components. The following statements hold after replacing $B$ by a Zariski-open neighborhood of $0$; for symplectic transport we subsequently work in a small complex analytic neighborhood.*\n\n1.  *For a sufficiently divisible and sufficiently large $N$, a full basis of $H^0(Y,\\mathcal L^N|_Y)$ can be extended to sections defining a relative projective embedding, with additional sections whose restrictions to $Y$ vanish. Consequently one can choose a relative Kähler form whose restriction to $Y$ is the projective form from that full basis, divided by $N$. If a complex torus $(\\mathbb C^*)^r$ acts holomorphically on $Y$ and its action is linearized on $\\mathcal L^N|_Y$, the basis may be chosen by weights, making the restricted form invariant under the compact torus $(S^1)^r$.*\n\n2.  *Let $K\\subset Y$ be compact and contained in the smooth submersion locus of $f$. For the chosen form there is, for all sufficiently small nonzero $t$, a symplectic embedding of a neighborhood of $K$ into the fiber $\\mathcal X_t$. If $K$ avoids a specified closed subset of $\\mathcal X$, the embedding can be chosen with image avoiding that subset.*\n\n3.  *If a compact group acts on the family over $B$ and the total form is invariant, this transport is equivariant. In particular, for a Hamiltonian circle action, it preserves moment functions up to an additive constant on each connected transport domain. If one transported point has the same prescribed moment value in both fibers, this constant is zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-343.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $f:\\mathcal X\\to B$ be a flat projective family over a smooth algebraic curve over $\\mathbb C$, with a marked point $0$, and let $\\mathcal L$ be a relatively ample rational polarization. The following statements hold after replacing $B$ by a Zariski-open neighborhood of $0$; for symplectic transport we subsequently work in a small complex analytic neighborhood.*\n\n1.  *For a sufficiently divisible and sufficiently large $N$, a full basis of $H^0(Y,\\mathcal L^N|_Y)$ can be extended to sections defining a relative projective embedding, with additional sections whose restrictions to $Y$ vanish. Consequently one can choose a relative Kähler form whose restriction to $Y$ is the projective form from that full basis, divided by $N$. If a complex torus $(\\mathbb C^*)^r$ acts holomorphically on $Y$ and its action is linearized on $\\mathcal L^N|_Y$, the basis may be chosen by weights, making the restricted form invariant under the compact torus $(S^1)^r$.*\n\n2.  *Let $K\\subset Y$ be compact and contained in the smooth submersion locus of $f$. If one transported point has the same prescribed moment value in both fibers, this constant is zero.",
      "explanation": "Compact parts of a smooth central component of a projective degeneration transport symplectically to nearby fibers, avoiding closed sets and respecting torus symmetry and moments.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-projective-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\mathcal X\\to B$ be a flat projective family over a smooth algebraic curve over $\\mathbb C$, with a marked point $0$",

      "id": "claim-projective-transfer",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a smooth projective manifold, $Z\\subset T$ a smooth closed submanifold of positive codimension, possibly disconnected, and $L$ an ample rational polarization. Let $$T'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bl}}_ZT,\\qquad\n Y=\\mathbb P_Z(\\mathbf1\\oplus N_{Z/T}),\\qquad\n D_\\infty=\\mathbb P_Z(N_{Z/T})\\subset Y.$$ Write $E_Z$ for the exceptional divisor on $T'$; when $Z$ is a divisor, $T'\\simeq T$ and $E_Z$ means $Z$. Suppose $d>0$ is rational and $$\\pi^*L-dE_Z\\quad\\hbox{on }T',\n \\qquad L|_Z+dU\\quad\\hbox{on }Y$$ are ample.*\n\n*Let $F\\subset T$ be a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth closed complex submanifolds. Assume the inverse image of $F\\cap Z$ under $Y\\to Z$ is also a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth complex submanifolds; the empty union is allowed. Let $\\omega_T$ be any Kähler form in class $L$, and let $\\omega_Y$ be a Kähler form in class $L|_Z+dU$, invariant under scalar rotation of the normal summand. Then every compact subset of $$Y\\setminus\\bigl(D_\\infty\\cup\\pi_Y^{-1}(F\\cap Z)\\bigr)$$ has a neighborhood admitting a symplectic embedding into $(T\\setminus F,\\omega_T)$, with its given form $\\omega_Y$. In particular any compact packing there transfers with neighborhood embeddings.",
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a smooth projective manifold, $Z\\subset T$ a smooth closed submanifold of positive codimension, possibly disconnected, and $L$ an ample rational polarization. Suppose $d>0$ is rational and $$\\pi^*L-dE_Z\\quad\\hbox{on }T',\n \\qquad L|_Z+dU\\quad\\hbox{on }Y$$ are ample.*\n\n*Let $F\\subset T$ be a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth closed complex submanifolds. Assume the inverse image of $F\\cap Z$ under $Y\\to Z$ is also a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth complex submanifolds; the empty union is allowed. Let $\\omega_T$ be any Kähler form in class $L$, and let $\\omega_Y$ be a Kähler form in class $L|_Z+dU$, invariant under scalar rotation of the normal summand. Then every compact subset of $$Y\\setminus\\bigl(D_\\infty\\cup\\pi_Y^{-1}(F\\cap Z)\\bigr)$$ has a neighborhood admitting a symplectic embedding into $(T\\setminus F,\\omega_T)$, with its given form $\\omega_Y$. In particular any compact packing there transfers with neighborhood embeddings.",
          "source_id": "PM-343.1"
        }
      ],

Let $$T'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bl}}_ZT,\\qquad\n Y=\\mathbb P_Z(\\mathbf1\\oplus N_{Z/T}),\\qquad\n D_\\infty=\\mathbb P_Z(N_{Z/T})\\subset Y.$$ Write $E_Z$ for the exceptional divisor on $T'$; when $Z$ is a divisor, $T'\\simeq T$ and $E_Z$ means $Z$. Suppose $d>0$ is rational and $$\\pi^*L-dE_Z\\quad\\hbox{on }T',\n \\qquad L|_Z+dU\\quad\\hbox{on }Y$$ are ample.*\n\n*Let $F\\subset T$ be a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth closed complex submanifolds. Assume the inverse image of $F\\cap Z$ under $Y\\to Z$ is also a finite union of pairwise disjoint smooth complex submanifolds; the empty union is allowed. Let $\\omega_T$ be any Kähler form in class $L$, and let $\\omega_Y$ be a Kähler form in class $L|_Z+dU$, invariant under scalar rotation of the normal summand. Then every compact subset of $$Y\\setminus\\bigl(D_\\infty\\cup\\pi_Y^{-1}(F\\cap Z)\\bigr)$$ has a neighborhood admitting a symplectic embedding into $(T\\setminus F,\\omega_T)$, with its given form $\\omega_Y$. In particular any compact packing there transfers with neighborhood embeddings.",
      "explanation": "Compact subsets of the normal-bundle component of the normal cone embed symplectically into the original manifold away from the chosen divisors.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-bundle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be an ample line bundle on a smooth projective manifold $Z$, equipped with a Hermitian metric of Chern form $\\sigma>0$. Suppose $$N=A^b\\oplus\\cdots\\oplus A^b$$ has $\\ell$ summands, where $b$ is a positive integer. If $d>0$ and $bd<1$, the class $\\sigma+dU$ on $Y=\\mathbb P_Z(\\mathbf1\\oplus N)$ has an invariant Kähler representative with the following affine description: $$\\label{geo:bundle-form}\n \\omega_Y=\\sigma+\\mathrm d\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{\\ell}p_j\\alpha_j\\right),\n \\qquad p_j\\ge0,\\quad \\sum_jp_j<d.$$ Here $\\alpha_j$ is the angular connection of period one on the $j$-th normal line, with curvature $-b\\sigma$, and the fiber coordinates have standard moments $p_j$. The expression $p_j\\alpha_j$ is smooth across its zero axis. For rational $d$, this also proves ampleness of the indicated rational polarization.*\n\n*If $Z=C$ is a curve, then over a compact bordered subsurface $\\Sigma$ the model, truncated on any compact moment polytope strictly inside $\\sum p_j<d$, admits a toric horizontal primitive as in Lemma <a href=\"#surf:packing\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"surf:packing\">9</a>, with $$\\kappa(p)=\\left(1-b\\sum_jp_j\\right)\\sigma.$$ For bordered exhaustions of $C$ minus finitely many points, the integrated areas converge uniformly on such a polytope to $$H(p)=(\\deg A)\\left(1-b\\sum_jp_j\\right).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-343.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge2$, let $o\\in\\mathbb P^n\\setminus H_\\infty$, and write $$T=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bl}}_o\\mathbb P^n,\\qquad H=\\pi^*c_1(\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P^n}(1)).$$ Let $E$ be the exceptional divisor and use $H_\\infty$ also for its proper transform.         "anchor": "stmt-app-quadrics",
        "exact_quote": "Let $N\\ge3$, and let $F\\subset\\mathbb P^N$ be a hyperplane. There is a smooth connected curve $C$, the complete intersection of $N-1$ quadrics, transverse to $F$.         "exact_quote": "There is an identification $D=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bl}}_{q_1,q_2}\\mathbb P^2$, for two distinct points $q_1,q_2$. Under this identification, $$\\label{six:quadric-classes}\n H|_D=2M-e_1-e_2,\\qquad E|_D=M-e_1-e_2.$$ Consequently $$\\label{six:normal-class}\n N_{D/T}=\\mathcal O_D(3M-e_1-e_2),\\qquad\n L|_D=3bM-s(e_1+e_2).$$ For every rational $0<c<s$, deformation to the normal cone of $D$ has ample component classes $$(1-2c)H-(a-c)E\n \\quad\\hbox{and}\\quad\n 3bM-s(e_1+e_2)+cU$$ on its strict component and on $$\\label{six:Y}\n Y=\\mathbb P_D\\bigl(\\mathbf1\\oplus\\mathcal O_D(3M-e_1-e_2)\\bigr),$$ respectively. The intersections of $D$ with $E$ and $H_\\infty$ are disjoint smooth curves; their images in $\\mathbb P^2$ are a line $\\ell$ and a smooth conic $Q$, both through $q_1,q_2$.",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose $s<d_1<b$ and $\\widehat V$ has an invariant Kähler form in the class $$\\label{six:Vhat-class}\n A=3bM+d_1U_1-s(\\widehat E_1+\\widehat E_2).$$ Normalize its first moment $p_1$ to be zero on the strict bottom section $D=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Bl}}_{q_1,q_2}\\mathbb P^2$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **capacity of a standard ball** ([source](#intro:section)): Thus $R$ is the capacity, the Euclidean radius is $\sqrt{R/\pi}$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}B^{2n}(R)=R^n/n!$.
- **toric function** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A function is *toric* if it depends only on the moments.
- **outer faces** ([source](#cmp:polytope)): We call the nonempty faces $b_\nu\cdot p=c_\nu$ the *outer faces*.
- **planar disk of area A** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Write $D(A)=\{z\in\mathbb C:\pi\|z\|^2<A\}$.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-volume-obstruction | sum of nth powers of source capacities | < | $R^n$ | positive real capacities, n at least three |
| q-pairwise-obstruction | sum of two distinct source capacities | < | $R$ | every pair of distinct balls |
| q-quadric-curve-degree | degree of the complete intersection curve of quadrics | = | $2^{N-1}$ | N at least three |
| q-quadric-curve-genus | genus of the complete intersection curve of quadrics | = | $1+(N-3)2^{N-2}$ | N at least three |
| q-six-limiting-area | limiting integrated base area over the punctured cubic | = | $9(b-p_1-p_2)-2(s-p_1-p_2)_+$ | uniform convergence on Delta along an exhaustion |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-rearrangement** [claim](#stmt-cmp-rearrangement-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-rearrangement-properties): Minimum-over-intervals formula gives continuity, convexity, monotonicity and integral preservation. -> properties of the coordinate rearrangement operators
- **step-planar-disks** [claim](#stmt-cmp-planar-disks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-planar-disks): Build diffeomorphisms by following disk boundaries, then correct areas with Moser's method. -> Hamiltonian isotopies moving centered disks to nested targets
- **step-planar-rearrangement** [claim](#stmt-cmp-disk-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-disk-realization): Approximate sublevel sets by nested smooth disks and apply the nested-disks lemma. -> planar Hamiltonian realization of the rearrangement
- **step-lifted** [claim](#stmt-cmp-lifted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-disk-realization-2): Lift the planar maps over slices with a cutoff and check outer-face commutation. -> lifted Hamiltonian diffeomorphism
- **step-comparison** [claim](#stmt-cmp-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-lifted): Contraction fixed point for the rearrangement map gives a uniform gap, then compose lifted maps. -> toric function K and Hamiltonian map q
- **step-primitives** [claim](#stmt-surf-primitive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-surf-primitive): Local explicit primitive in a coordinate disk, then partition of unity and chains of disks. -> linear primitive operator
- **step-moser-faces** [claim](#stmt-surf-moser) / [proof](#proof-stmt-surf-moser): Solve the Moser equation and show trajectories cannot cross outer levels. -> Moser isotopy preserving the product region
- **step-surface-packing** [claim](#stmt-surf-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-surf-packing): Five steps: retain gap, place area on an annulus, build exact path with layers, verify relative Moser conditions, embed a ball in each layer. -> disjoint packing in the surface model
- **step-relative-kahler-moser** [claim](#stmt-geo-relative-moser) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-relative-moser): Linear path of Kähler forms with a first-jet normal correction to keep submanifolds invariant. -> isotopy preserving submanifolds
- **step-projective-transfer** [claim](#stmt-geo-projective-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-projective-transfer): Lift sections by relative Serre vanishing, build a relative Kähler form, use horizontal lifts for transport. -> symplectic transport to nearby fibers
- **step-normal-cone-transfer** [claim](#stmt-geo-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-transfer): Deformation to the normal cone, ampleness on components, then transfer and Moser identification. -> embedding of normal component regions into the target
- **step-bundle-form** [claim](#stmt-geo-bundle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-bundle): Explicit potential on the affine chart and moment coordinates. -> invariant Kähler form with affine description
- **step-shell** [claim](#stmt-geo-shell) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-shell): Symplectic cut of projective space times the plane, identify the complement. -> shell symplectomorphism
- **step-quadrics** [claim](#stmt-app-quadrics) / [proof](#proof-stmt-app-quadrics): Bertini, Koszul resolution and adjunction, then Rees algebra embedding for ampleness. -> curve data and ample classes
- **step-small** [claim](#stmt-app-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-app-small): Choose slack parameters, verify the surface packing hypotheses on the quadric model, transfer. -> packing for capacities below one half
- **step-large** [claim](#stmt-app-large) / [proof](#proof-stmt-app-large): Two normal constructions over a hyperplane and a curve, volume computation, transfer into the shell. -> packing with a large ball
- **step-six-quadric** [claim](#stmt-six-quadric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-quadric): Projection from the point identifies the quadric with a two-point blowup. -> classes on the quadric blowup
- **step-six-cut** [claim](#stmt-six-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-cut): Fixed-level analysis and Kähler reduction. -> cut model equal to the projective bundle
- **step-six-ampleness** [claim](#stmt-six-cubic-ampleness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-cubic-ampleness): Toric curve tests and invariant cycles. -> ample component classes
- **step-six-weights** [claim](#stmt-six-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-weights): Expand in the projective coordinates and use degree bounds on a genus one curve. -> weight spaces and basepoint-freeness
- **step-six-toric** [claim](#stmt-six-toric-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-toric-model): Legendre transform of the potential, log mass at marked points, Dini's theorem. -> uniform limiting area profile
- **step-six-moment** [claim](#stmt-six-moment-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-moment-transfer): Equivariant parallel transport with normalization on the fixed section. -> moment-preserving transfer
- **step-six-large** [claim](#stmt-six-large) / [proof](#proof-stmt-six-large): Choose parameters, verify surface packing hypotheses for the limiting density, transfer, cut, and add the central ball. -> six-dimensional packing with a large ball
- **step-main** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-intro-main): Necessity from volume and two-ball obstruction; sufficiency by cases using the small and large propositions. -> complete classification

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-embedding-iff-volume-and-pairwise**: For integer dimension parameter n at least three and any finite list of positive capacities, disjoint symplectic embedding of the closed balls into the open target ball exists exactly when the volume inequality and the pairwise capacity inequality both hold strictly.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-low-dimension**: Apply the two inequalities as a packing criterion in dimension parameter one or two. -> The criterion is only proved for n at least three; in lower dimension further obstructions exist, so the check can accept packings that do not exist.
- **anti-nonpositive-capacity**: Feed zero or negative capacities to the decision routine and trust its answer. -> The equivalence is stated only for positive real capacities; other inputs are outside the theorem and the answer carries no meaning.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The paper's embedding constructions are not finite procedures, but Theorem 1 turns existence into a finite exact check on rational capacities: a power sum and a pairwise sum compared with the target. Panel 3 implements only this decision check; it does not build embeddings.

```python
# INPUTS: three whitespace-separated groups: integer n, target capacity R, then k capacities, all as exact rationals such as 1/3.
# PRECONDITIONS: n >= 3, R > 0, k >= 1, every capacity > 0.
# OUTPUTS: the single word YES if both strict inequalities of the criterion hold, otherwise NO.
# TIME: sorting plus one power sum, O(k log k) exact rational operations.
# SPACE: O(k) rationals.
# ARITHMETIC: exact Fraction arithmetic, no floating point.
# FAILURE: exit status 2 when a precondition fails.
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n = int(data[0])
    R = Fraction(data[1])
    rs = sorted(Fraction(x) for x in data[2:])
    if n < 3 or R <= 0 or len(rs) < 1 or rs[0] <= 0:
        sys.exit(2)
    ok = sum(r**n for r in rs) < R**n
    if len(rs) >= 2 and rs[-1]+rs[-2] >= R:
        ok = False
    print("YES" if ok else "NO")


main()
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-dimension-at-least-three** (hypothesis): The criterion is proved only for n at least three and finitely many balls. It is not a full packing criterion in lower dimension, where further obstructions remain. Blocked use: ['applying the two inequalities as a complete packing test in real dimension two or four', 'treating a pass of the inequalities as proof of packing when n is below three']
- **limit-comparison-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The Hamiltonian comparison step needs a continuous concave profile with positive mean that is also positive outside a radial core. A profile without concavity or without that positivity is outside the lemma. Blocked use: ['running the comparison on a non-concave unused-area profile', 'assuming the integral surplus alone gives a pointwise gap']
- **limit-existence-not-construction** (nonconstructive): The packing results assert existence of embeddings defined on neighborhoods of the closed balls. The proofs use Moser isotopies, fixed points and algebraic degenerations, so no explicit embedding or running time is supplied. Blocked use: ['claiming an algorithm that outputs an explicit symplectic embedding', 'quoting a complexity bound for building the packing']
- **limit-linear-primitive-support** (formal_scope): Relative area primitives are produced only for zero-integral two-forms supported in a fixed compact subset of the interior of a bordered surface. Forms with nonzero total integral or support reaching the boundary are outside the lemma. Blocked use: ['applying the primitive operator to forms with nonzero integral', 'using the operator across the surface boundary']
- **limit-quadric-curve-dimension** (hypothesis): The complete-intersection curve of quadrics is constructed for ambient projective dimension N at least three, with a transverse hyperplane. The genus formula and ample range are tied to this setting. Blocked use: ['using the curve data for N below three', 'using the ample range without the stated hyperplane transversality']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 343 (`lean/docs/343.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Symplectic Ball Packings in Higher Dimensions](../../preprints/Symplectic-Ball-Packings-in-Higher-Dimensions-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves Siegel and Yao's ball-packing criterion in every symplectic dimension $2n$ with $n\ge3$. For $k\ge1$ closed standard balls of positive capacities $R_1,\ldots,R_k$, disjoint symplectic embeddings into the interior of a ball of capacity $R$ exist exactly when
> $\sum_i R_i^n<R^n$ and $R_i+R_j<R$ for all $i\ne j$.
> Capacity is $\pi$ times squared Euclidean radius, and each embedding is defined on a neighborhood of its closed source ball. The linked statements include both the full equivalence and the necessity direction.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `Phase` | abbrev |
| `capacity` | def |
| `closedBall` | def |

| `standardForm` | def |

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace HigherDimensionalBallPacking
open scoped ContDiff

abbrev Phase (n : ℕ) := Fin n → ℂ

noncomputable def capacity {n : ℕ} (z : Phase n) : ℝ :=
  Real.pi * ∑ j, Complex.normSq (z j)

def closedBall (n : ℕ) (r : ℝ) : Set (Phase n) := {z | capacity z ≤ r}

def openBall (n : ℕ) (r : ℝ) : Set (Phase n) := {z | capacity z < r}

noncomputable def standardForm {n : ℕ} (u v : Phase n) : ℝ :=
  ∑ j, ((u j).re * (v j).im - (u j).im * (v j).re)

def SymplecticOn {n : ℕ} (U : Set (Phase n)) (f : Phase n → Phase n) : Prop :=
  IsOpen U ∧ ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ f U ∧
    Topology.IsEmbedding (fun x : U => f x) ∧
    ∀ x ∈ U, ∀ u v : Phase n,
      standardForm (fderiv ℝ f x u) (fderiv ℝ f x v) = standardForm u v

def HasPacking (n k : ℕ) (R : ℝ) (r : Fin k → ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (U : Fin k → Set (Phase n)) (f : Fin k → Phase n → Phase n),
    (∀ i, closedBall n (r i) ⊆ U i ∧ SymplecticOn (U i) (f i) ∧
      Set.MapsTo (f i) (closedBall n (r i)) (openBall n R)) ∧
    (∀ i j, i ≠ j →
      Disjoint (f i '' closedBall n (r i)) (f j '' closedBall n (r j)))

def PackingInequalities (n k : ℕ) (R : ℝ) (r : Fin k → ℝ) : Prop :=
  (∑ i, r i ^ n) < R ^ n ∧ ∀ i j, i ≠ j → r i + r j < R

theorem main_theorem (n k : ℕ) (hn : 3 ≤ n) (hk : 1 ≤ k)
    (R : ℝ) (r : Fin k → ℝ) (hR : 0 < R) (hr : ∀ i, 0 < r i) :
    HasPacking n k R r ↔ PackingInequalities n k R r := by
  sorry

end HigherDimensionalBallPacking

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that, for all integers <i>n</i> ≥ 3 and <i>k</i> ≥ 1 and all positive real capacities $`R_1,\ldots,R_k`$, the closed standard symplectic $`2n`$-balls of these capacities embed disjointly into the interior of a ball of capacity <i>R</i> &gt; 0 if and only if

$`\displaystyle \sum_{i=1}^k R_i^n\lt R^n, \qquad R_i+R_j\lt R\quad(i\ne j).`$

Capacity is <i>π</i> times the squared Euclidean radius, and each embedding is defined on a neighborhood of its closed source ball. This proves Siegel and Yao's Conjecture A.

<a id="intro:section"></a>
### Introduction

Symplectic ball packing asks how much of a volume-preserving embedding problem is constrained by symplectic rigidity. For $R>0$, write $$B^{2n}(R)=
 \left\{z\in\mathbb C^n:\pi\sum_{j=1}^n|z_j|^2\le R\right\},
 \qquad
 \omega_0=\sum_{j=1}^n\mathrm dx_j\wedge\mathrm dy_j.$$ Thus $R$ is the capacity, the Euclidean radius is $\sqrt{R/\pi}$, and $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}B^{2n}(R)=R^n/n!$. We ask when finitely many such balls can be embedded symplectically into the interior of a target ball. An embedding of a closed ball is understood to be a symplectic embedding defined on an open neighborhood of it. For a finite disjoint union, the compact images of the closed balls must be pairwise disjoint.

<a id="stmt-intro-main"></a>
<div id="intro:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $n\ge3$ and $k\ge1$ be integers. For positive real numbers $R,R_1,\ldots,R_k$, there exists a symplectic embedding $$\bigsqcup_{i=1}^k B^{2n}(R_i)
 \hookrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{int}}B^{2n}(R)$$ if and only if <a id="intro:inequalities"></a>$$\label{intro:inequalities}
 \sum_{i=1}^k R_i^n<R^n,
 \qquad
 R_i+R_j<R\quad (1\le i<j\le k).$$*

</div>

The first inequality is the volume obstruction. The second is Gromov’s two-ball obstruction <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Gromov1985](#ref-Gromov1985)]; it expresses a restriction invisible to volume alone. Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a> proves that these obstructions are complete in every real dimension at least six, resolving Siegel and Yao’s Conjecture A positively <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[SiegelYao2025](#ref-SiegelYao2025)]. For one ball the pairwise condition is vacuous. The strict inequalities reflect the closed-source, open-target convention.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Packing rigidity and stability

Gromov’s pseudoholomorphic-curve method gives the two-ball obstruction in every dimension, and further packing obstructions in dimension four <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Gromov1985](#ref-Gromov1985), Section 0.3.B]. McDuff and Polterovich developed the connection with algebraic geometry <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[McDuffPolterovich](#ref-McDuffPolterovich)]. Explicit constructions form a complementary strand: Traynor developed symplectic packing constructions <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Traynor](#ref-Traynor)], and Schlenk developed planar constructions and higher-dimensional folding methods <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[SchlenkHand](#ref-SchlenkHand), [SchlenkBook](#ref-SchlenkBook)]. In real dimension four, additional obstructions remain essential, so the two inequalities in Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a> do not give a criterion there.

Biran’s work <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Biran1997](#ref-Biran1997), [Biran1999](#ref-Biran1999)] established packing stability: on a closed symplectic four-manifold with rational symplectic class, every sufficiently large number of equal balls can fill an arbitrarily large fraction of the volume. Buse and Hind extended equal-ball stability to higher-dimensional balls and projective spaces and then to all closed rational symplectic manifolds <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BuseHind2011](#ref-BuseHind2011), [BuseHind2013](#ref-BuseHind2013)]. For unequal balls, Buse, Hind, and Opshtein proved strong packing stability on every closed symplectic four-manifold, with all capacities required to be sufficiently small <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BuseHindOpshtein2016](#ref-BuseHindOpshtein2016)]. These results identify regimes in which volume suffices; the question here concerns every finite collection of arbitrary capacities in a ball.

Siegel and Yao formulate this higher-dimensional question and also study stabilized packing problems <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[SiegelYao2025](#ref-SiegelYao2025)]. Their theorems on products of four-dimensional balls with a fixed closed symplectic surface retain four-dimensional packing obstructions. Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a> concerns balls themselves, with arbitrary unequal capacities and arbitrary finite numbers of components.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### From a mean area inequality to ball embeddings

Normalize the target capacity to one. The common construction uses a surface of positive genus as a base and a toric domain in $\mathbb C^m$, where $m=n-1$, as a fiber. The fiber is specified by a downward polytope $\Delta\subset\mathbb R_{\ge0}^m$ in the moment coordinates $p_j=\pi|z_j|^2$: lowering any coordinate keeps a point in $\Delta$. Write $H(p)>0$ for the limiting total area of the base at fixed moment $p$, obtained by exhausting a punctured surface by compact bordered pieces. A ball of auxiliary capacity $r_i$, chosen strictly larger than the corresponding requested capacity, requires the area profile $$\left(r_i-\sum_{j=1}^m p_j\right)_+,
 \qquad x_+=\max(x,0).$$ The models contain each auxiliary simplex $\sum_jp_j\le r_i$ strictly away from their noncoordinate boundary faces. The volume inequality becomes positivity of the integral of the unused-area profile $$P(p)=H(p)-\sum_i\left(r_i-\sum_jp_j\right)_+$$ over $\Delta$. This integral condition need not make $P$ positive at each moment, which is the obstacle to simply stacking the balls.

Section <a href="#cmp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:section">2</a> overcomes that obstacle by Hamiltonian comparison. Under concavity of $P$ and positivity outside a radial core of $\Delta$, Lemma <a href="#cmp:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:comparison">2</a> constructs a smooth toric function $K$ and a Hamiltonian map $q$ such that $$(K-P)\circ q<K.$$ Coordinatewise increasing rearrangements of convex functions preserve their integrals and lead to a contraction whose fixed point gives a uniform gap. Smooth planar disk maps realize the rearrangements with errors smaller than that gap. Near each noncoordinate boundary face, the generating Hamiltonians commute with its defining moment function; this is the boundary control needed in the next step.

Section <a href="#surf:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="surf:section">3</a> converts the comparison into embeddings. A handle provides two transverse annuli and a closed one-form with a nonzero period. These allow the Hamiltonian change of fiber coordinates to be inserted into an exact symplectic deformation. The uniform gap leaves disjoint layers whose area profiles contain the requested balls. The relative Moser argument in Lemma <a href="#surf:packing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="surf:packing">9</a> returns the packing to the original model, preserving its boundary conditions. The construction uses the limiting concave function $P$; the integrated areas of finite bordered models need only converge uniformly.

The connection between packing and Hamiltonian-group structure described in Edtmair’s abstracts <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[EdtmairPerfectness2025](#ref-EdtmairPerfectness2025), [EdtmairPacking2025](#ref-EdtmairPacking2025)] was one motivation for the commutator viewpoint in this construction. The present one-handle comparison and exact deformation are constructed locally, rather than obtained from those results.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The geometric models and the three cases

To apply this mechanism we need models carrying nearly all the available volume over a surface with a handle. The use of a polarization to expose standard symplectic regions has precedents in Biran’s decomposition method <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Biran2001](#ref-Biran2001)] and Opshtein’s singular polarizations and ellipsoid packings <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Opshtein2013](#ref-Opshtein2013)]. Witt Nyström showed that modified deformations to the normal cone can place arbitrarily nearly all the volume of a compact Kähler manifold in a normal-bundle component <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[WittNystrom2024](#ref-WittNystrom2024)]. Here explicit toric models over positive-genus curves supply the area profiles needed by the surface packing lemma. Section <a href="#geo:devices" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:devices">4</a> develops the relative transfer statements that take compact packings from these models back to the target. Its deformation and reduction tools are Moser’s method <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Moser1965](#ref-Moser1965)], symplectic cuts <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Lerman1995](#ref-Lerman1995)], and their Kähler interpretation <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BurnsGuilleminLerman2002](#ref-BurnsGuilleminLerman2002)]; the relative and equivariant details are proved where needed.

The pairwise inequalities leave three cases, summarized in Figure <a href="#fig:proof-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-flow">1</a>. If every requested capacity is below $1/2$, Section <a href="#app:applications" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:applications">5</a> degenerates $\mathbb P^n$ along a complete intersection of quadrics. Its curve has positive genus for every $n\ge3$, and its normal model approaches the full volume of the target. Proposition <a href="#app:small" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:small">16</a> applies the surface packing lemma to this model.

If a requested capacity is at least $1/2$, it is the unique such capacity. Reserve a slightly larger central ball and pack the remaining balls into its exterior shell. For $n\ge4$, a hyperplane degeneration followed by a curve degeneration in its base gives a positive-genus model of that shell. This is Proposition <a href="#app:large" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:large">17</a>, also in Section <a href="#app:applications" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:applications">5</a>.

In complex dimension three, the same procedure would use a plane conic, which has genus zero. Section <a href="#six:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="six:section">6</a> instead starts from a quadric through the blowup point and uses a further degeneration along a plane cubic with two marked points. If the reserved capacity is $a>1/2$, set $s=1-a$ and $b=(2-a)/3$. The resulting integrated base area is $$H_0(p)=9(b-p_1-p_2)-2(s-p_1-p_2)_+.$$ Each marked point contributes one of the two subtracted logarithmic masses. This concave density gives the required shell volume, while exact preservation of the first circle moment $p_1$ under transport permits a lower Kähler cut. The cubic family also supplies the invariant Kähler form needed for that cut. Proposition <a href="#six:large" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="six:large">24</a> completes this last case, and Section <a href="#app:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:completion">7</a> assembles the theorem.

All requested capacities remain arbitrary positive real numbers. Rational auxiliary polarizations and compact truncations are chosen with strict slack. Every transfer is defined on a neighborhood of the relevant compact set, so the construction retains embeddings of the whole closed source balls under finite composition.

<figure id="fig:proof-flow">

<figcaption>The three geometric applications feed the same comparison and surface-packing mechanism. Capacities are normalized by the target capacity. The six-dimensional transfer also uses the lower Kähler cut. The one-ball case is immediate.</figcaption>
</figure>

```text
- Top level: Three rectangular boxes side by side:
  - Left box text: "All capacities < 1/2 / Complete intersection of quadrics / Proposition 5.2"
  - Middle box text: "One capacity $\ge 1/2$, $n \ge 4$ / Two normal models / Proposition 5.3"
  - Right box text: "One capacity $\ge 1/2$, $n = 3$ / Cubic with two marks / Proposition 6.7"
- Arrows: Three downward arrows, one from each top box, converging to the second level box.
- Second level: A wide rectangular box:
  - Text: "Toric models over a surface with a handle: limiting unused-area profile $P$: concave, positive mean, positive outside a radial core"
- Arrow: A downward arrow pointing to the third level box.
- Third level: A rectangular box:
  - Text: "Hamiltonian comparison $\longrightarrow$ disjoint ball layers / Lemmas 2.1 and 3.3"
- Arrow: A downward arrow pointing to the fourth level box.
- Fourth level: A rectangular box:
  - Text: "Symplectic transfer to the target ball or shell; add the central ball in the two large-ball cases"
```


<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions and the easy direction

We use the Hamiltonian convention $\iota_{X_H}\omega=-\mathrm dH$. On $\mathbb C^m$, moment and angle coordinates are $$p_j=\pi|z_j|^2,\qquad
 \theta_j=\frac{\arg z_j}{2\pi},\qquad
 \omega_0=\sum_j\mathrm dp_j\wedge\mathrm d\theta_j.$$ Moment formulas are always interpreted in the original smooth complex coordinates at a coordinate axis. A function is *toric* if it depends only on the moments. Projective spaces and line bundles use Chern class units: a projective line in the unit class has area one. Projectivizations parametrize lines, and $U=c_1(\mathcal O(1))$ denotes the dual tautological class. We normalize $\mathrm d^c$ by $\mathrm d^c\log|z|^2=\mathrm d\theta$ away from zero; consequently $\mathrm d\mathrm d^c\log|z|^2$ has a unit atom at zero.

<details id="proof-stmt-intro-main"><summary>Proof of stmt-intro-main</summary>

*Necessity in Theorem <a href="#intro:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:main">1</a>.* The union of the finitely many compact images is contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{int}}B^{2n}(\sigma)$ for some $\sigma<R$. Volume preservation gives $\sum_iR_i^n\le\sigma^n<R^n$. Gromov’s two-ball obstruction <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Gromov1985](#ref-Gromov1985), Section 0.3.B], in the capacity convention of <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[SiegelYao2025](#ref-SiegelYao2025), Theorem 1.1], gives $R_i+R_j\le\sigma<R$ for every pair. ◻

</details>

Conjugating the source and target by the same dilation reduces sufficiency to target capacity one; the conformal factors in the two pullbacks cancel. We henceforth use this normalization. A single ball of capacity less than one embeds by inclusion. The proof for multiple balls is completed after the three geometric applications, in Section <a href="#app:completion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="app:completion">7</a>.

<a id="cmp:section"></a>
### Hamiltonian comparison on a toric domain

Our goal is to turn a positive mean area surplus into a strict pointwise inequality after a Hamiltonian change of fiber coordinates. The construction must also preserve the boundary moments needed by the surface deformation in Section <a href="#surf:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="surf:section">3</a>.

<a id="cmp:polytope"></a>
Let $$\label{cmp:polytope}
 \Delta=\{p\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^m:
             b_\nu\cdot p\le c_\nu,\ 1\le\nu\le N\},
 \qquad b_\nu\in\mathbb R_{\ge0}^m,\quad c_\nu>0,$$ be compact and full dimensional, with $m\ge1$. We call the nonempty faces $b_\nu\cdot p=c_\nu$ the *outer faces*. The coordinate faces $p_j=0$ are not outer faces. Put $$V=\{z\in\mathbb C^m:p(z)\in\Delta\},\qquad
 p_j(z)=\pi|z_j|^2,$$ with its standard symplectic form $\omega_V$. We freely regard functions on $\Delta$ as toric functions on $V$. Smooth maps, Hamiltonians, and differential forms on these compact sets will always be defined on neighborhoods. No simplicity or rationality assumption on $\Delta$ is needed.

<a id="stmt-cmp-comparison"></a>
<div id="cmp:comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Hamiltonian comparison). *Let $P:\Delta\to\mathbb R$ be continuous and concave. Suppose that its Lebesgue mean $\overline P$ is positive and, for some $0<\tau<1$, <a id="cmp:outer-positive"></a>$$\label{cmp:outer-positive}
 \inf_{\Delta\setminus\tau\Delta}P>0.$$ Then there are a smooth toric function $K$ and a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism $q:V\to V$ such that <a id="cmp:conclusion"></a>$$\label{cmp:conclusion}
 (K-P)(qu)<K(u)\qquad(u\in V).$$ The map $q$ admits a Hamiltonian isotopy from the identity preserving $V$, whose Hamiltonians Poisson commute with $b_\nu\cdot p$ near each corresponding outer face, uniformly in time.*

</div>

We separate the rearrangement inequality from its symplectic realization. First we define the rearrangement operator and state the realization property needed by the proof. The fixed-point argument then produces a uniform gap; the remaining subsection proves the realization property with an arbitrarily small error and the required boundary control.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Coordinate rearrangements

<a id="cmp:interval-formula"></a>
For a continuous convex function $f:[0,1]\to\mathbb R$, its increasing rearrangement with respect to Lebesgue measure at $h\in[0,1]$ can be written as $$\label{cmp:interval-formula}
 g(h)=\min_{0\le a\le1-h}\max\{f(a),f(a+h)\}.$$ This also defines the endpoint values $g(0)=\min f$ and $g(1)=\max f$.

For a continuous convex function $F$ on $\Delta$, let $R_jF$ denote increasing rearrangement in $p_j$, with the other moments $y=(p_i)_{i\ne j}$ fixed. The slice is $[0,L(y)]$, where $$L(y)=
 \min_{\nu:b_{\nu j}>0}
 \frac{c_\nu-\sum_{i\ne j}b_{\nu i}y_i}{b_{\nu j}}.$$ At least one such bound exists by compactness of $\Delta$. The function $L$ is continuous, concave, and piecewise affine on the projected polytope. Define $R_jF$ by the rescaled version of <a href="#cmp:interval-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cmp:interval-formula">[cmp:interval-formula]</a>.

<a id="stmt-cmp-rearrangement-properties"></a>
<div id="cmp:rearrangement-properties" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Each $R_j$ maps continuous convex functions on $\Delta$ to continuous convex functions. It preserves Lebesgue integrals, order, and addition of constants, and is nonexpansive in uniform norm. Its output is nondecreasing in $p_j$; moreover it preserves being nondecreasing in any other coordinate. Consequently $R=R_m\cdots R_1$ has coordinatewise nondecreasing output.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cmp-rearrangement-properties"><summary>Proof of stmt-cmp-rearrangement-properties</summary>

*Proof.* Continuity follows from the minimum-over-intervals formula. At a slice of length tending to zero, uniform continuity of $F$ gives the same conclusion. For convexity, choose minimizing intervals of lengths $h_0,h_1$ on two slices. Their convex combination is an allowed interval of length $(1-t)h_0+th_1$ on the intermediate slice. Convexity of $F$ bounds its two endpoint values by the corresponding convex combinations, which proves convexity of the rearrangement.

The minimum formula is nondecreasing in the interval length. If $F$ is nondecreasing in another coordinate, lowering that coordinate preserves every allowed interval, by the nonnegative coefficients in <a href="#cmp:polytope" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cmp:polytope">[cmp:polytope]</a>, and can only lower its endpoint values. This proves preservation of the previous monotonicity properties.

On each slice, increasing rearrangement preserves the distribution of values, including flat sublevels, and hence the integral. Integrate this identity in the other coordinates. Order and translation invariance follow directly from the minimum formula. Applying these to $G-\left\lVert F-G\right\rVert_\infty\le F\le G+\left\lVert F-G\right\rVert_\infty$ proves nonexpansiveness. ◻

</details>

The following lemma realizes one coordinate rearrangement up to a prescribed error. Its proof, given after the proof of Lemma <a href="#cmp:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:comparison">2</a>, uses smooth families of planar disk maps.

<a id="stmt-cmp-lifted"></a>
<div id="cmp:lifted" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Lifted rearrangement). *For every continuous convex $F:\Delta\to\mathbb R$, every coordinate $j$, and every $\varepsilon>0$, there is a Hamiltonian diffeomorphism $q_j:V\to V$ such that $$F\circ q_j\le R_jF+\varepsilon.$$ Its isotopy preserves $V$, and its Hamiltonians Poisson commute with every outer-face moment near the corresponding face.*

</div>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### A fixed point with a uniform gap

<details id="proof-stmt-cmp-lifted"><summary>Proof of stmt-cmp-lifted</summary>

<a id="cmp:mean"></a>
*Proof of Lemma <a href="#cmp:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:comparison">2</a>.* The continuous convex functions on $\Delta$ form a complete metric space in the uniform norm. Since $-P$ is convex, Lemma <a href="#cmp:rearrangement-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:rearrangement-properties">3</a> implies that, for $0<\lambda<1$, the map $$F\longmapsto R(\lambda F-P)$$ is a contraction of this space, with constant $\lambda$. Let $K_\lambda$ be its fixed point. Integral preservation gives $$\label{cmp:mean}
 \overline K_\lambda
 =-\frac{\overline P}{1-\lambda}.$$ The function $K_\lambda$ is coordinatewise nondecreasing; in particular $K_\lambda(0)=\min_\Delta K_\lambda$.

<a id="cmp:negative-maximum"></a>
Choose $$0<d<\min\left\{\overline P,\,
               \inf_{\Delta\setminus\tau\Delta}P\right\}.$$ We claim that, for $\lambda$ sufficiently close to one, $$\label{cmp:negative-maximum}
 M_\lambda:=\max_\Delta K_\lambda
 <-\frac d{1-\lambda}.$$ Suppose otherwise for such a $\lambda$. Then $\lambda M_\lambda\le M_\lambda+d$, and consequently $\lambda K_\lambda-P<M_\lambda$ on the outer region. For $p\in\tau\Delta$, write $p=\tau v$ with $v\in\Delta$. Convexity, $K_\lambda(0)\le\overline K_\lambda$, and <a href="#cmp:mean" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cmp:mean">[cmp:mean]</a> give $$K_\lambda(p)
 \le \tau M_\lambda-
       \frac{(1-\tau)\overline P}{1-\lambda}
 \le M_\lambda-
       \frac{(1-\tau)(\overline P-d)}{1-\lambda}.$$ It follows that $$\lambda K_\lambda(p)-P(p)
 \le M_\lambda+d
 -\frac{\lambda(1-\tau)(\overline P-d)}{1-\lambda}
 -\min_\Delta P<M_\lambda$$ when $\lambda$ is close enough to one. The choices here depend only on the displayed constants, so the bound is uniform on both regions. We have shown $\max_\Delta(\lambda K_\lambda-P)<M_\lambda$, contradicting the fixed-point equation and $\max RF\le\max F$. This proves <a href="#cmp:negative-maximum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cmp:negative-maximum">[cmp:negative-maximum]</a>.

Fix such a $\lambda$. Since $(1-\lambda)K_\lambda\le-d$, order preservation and translation invariance give <a id="cmp:rearranged-gap"></a>$$\label{cmp:rearranged-gap}
 R(K_\lambda-P)
 \le R(\lambda K_\lambda-P)-d=K_\lambda-d.$$ Set $F_0=K_\lambda-P$ and $F_j=R_jF_{j-1}$. All these functions are continuous and convex. Use Lemma <a href="#cmp:lifted" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:lifted">4</a> to choose $q_j$ with $$F_{j-1}\circ q_j\le F_j+\varepsilon_j,
 \qquad \sum_j\varepsilon_j<d/2.$$ In the order $q=q_1\circ\cdots\circ q_m$, successive substitution of these inequalities yields $$(K_\lambda-P)\circ q
 \le R(K_\lambda-P)+\sum_j\varepsilon_j
 <K_\lambda-d/2.$$ Each constituent isotopy preserves $V$ and preserves every outer-face moment on a sufficiently small collar. Choose common threshold collars $c_\nu-\epsilon_\nu< b_\nu\cdot p\le c_\nu$ for the finitely many maps. Such collars are invariant because the moment is constant along each flow there. Run the isotopy for $q_m$ first, then that for $q_{m-1}$ on its output, and continue in this order. Each stage uses one of the original generating Hamiltonians and retains its commutation with every face moment. Reparametrization makes the concatenated path smooth and preserves these commutation relations.

Finally approximate $K_\lambda$ uniformly on $\Delta$ by a smooth function $K(p)$, defined on a neighborhood. Polynomial approximation is sufficient. An approximation error smaller than $d/4$ changes the last comparison by less than $d/2$, leaving $(K-P)\circ q<K$. This proves the lemma. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Realization by Hamiltonian maps

We now prove the realization lemma used above. Related constructions prescribe planar symplectic maps by nested equal-area curves; see <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[SchlenkFolding](#ref-SchlenkFolding), Lemma 3.2]. We need smooth parameter dependence and relative Hamiltonian control as well.

Write $D(A)=\{z\in\mathbb C:\pi|z|^2<A\}$. A smooth family of embedded closed disks means a family of smooth embeddings of the closed unit disk, smooth also in the parameters on an open neighborhood of their parameter set.

<a id="stmt-cmp-planar-disks"></a>
<div id="cmp:planar-disks" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Nested disks). *Let $Y$ be a compact convex subset of a Euclidean space, let $0<a_1<\cdots<a_l<A$, and let $$E_1(y)\Subset\mathop{\mathrm{int}}E_2(y)\Subset\cdots
       \Subset\mathop{\mathrm{int}}E_l(y)\Subset D(A)
 \qquad(y\in Y)$$ be smooth families of embedded closed disks of areas $a_1,\ldots,a_l$. There is a smooth family of Hamiltonian isotopies of $D(A)$, supported in a common compact subset, whose endpoint maps carry each centered disk of area $a_j$ onto $E_j(y)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cmp-planar-disks"><summary>Proof of stmt-cmp-planar-disks</summary>

*Proof.* First construct ordinary orientation-preserving diffeomorphisms with these properties. At a fixed parameter $y_0$, a disk embedding can be shrunk within its image, straightened while small, moved through the ambient disk, and expanded to another prescribed disk. The velocity along its moving boundary extends to a vector field in a tubular neighborhood and then, by a cutoff, to a compactly supported ambient field. The resulting flow extends the disk isotopy. Apply this construction first to the outermost boundary and then to each successive boundary inside its already positioned outer disk. This gives a compactly supported diffeomorphism $\phi_{y_0}$, isotopic to the identity, carrying the centered disks to the prescribed ones.

For other parameters, follow the disk boundaries along the segment $y_0+t(y-y_0)$. Their velocities extend in disjoint tubular neighborhoods of the finitely many boundaries.
